∎
B.11 Preliminaries for Theorem 23
Here, Nesterov and Polyak (2006, Lemma 1), Nesterov and Polyak (2006, Lemma 2), Nesterov and Polyak (2006, Lemma 3), Nesterov and Polyak (2006, Lemma 4), and Nesterov and Polyak (2006, Lemma 5), correspond to Lemmata 10, 14, 15, 16 and 18, respectively, proven for Diag⁡(∇2f​(𝐱))\Diag(\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x})) in place of ∇2f​(𝐱)\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}). In addition, Lemma 17 is proven providing details not included in (Nesterov and Polyak, 2006, Section 2).
Let the level set
𝔏⁡(c)={𝐱∈ℝd:f⁡(𝐱)≤c},\mathfrak{L}(c)=\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\colon f(\mathbf{x})\leq c\},
(177)
and assume that F⊆𝔏⁡(f⁡(𝐱0))\pazocal{F}\subseteq\mathfrak{L}(f(\mathbf{x}_{0})). Let
f^(𝐱,𝐲)=f(𝐱)+∇f(𝐱)T(𝐲−𝐱)+12(𝐲−𝐱)TDiag(∇2f(𝐱))(𝐲−𝐱)+M6‖𝐲−𝐱‖23,\hat{f}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})=f(\mathbf{x})+\nabla f(\mathbf{x})^{T}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{x})+\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{x})^{T}\Diag(\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}))(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{x})+\frac{M}{6}\left\lVert\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}^{3},
(178)
TM​(𝐱)=arg​min𝐲⁡f^​(𝐱,𝐲),T_{M}(\mathbf{x})=\argmin_{\mathbf{y}}\hat{f}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}),
(179)
and
f¯M​(𝐱)=min𝐲⁡f^​(𝐱,𝐲).\bar{f}_{M}(\mathbf{x})=\min_{\mathbf{y}}\hat{f}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}).
(180)
That is,
f¯M​(𝐱)=f^​(𝐱,TM​(𝐱)).\bar{f}_{M}(\mathbf{x})=\hat{f}(\mathbf{x},T_{M}(\mathbf{x})).
(181)
To compute TM​(𝐱)T_{M}(\mathbf{x}) in (179), we solve ∇𝐲f^​(𝐱,𝐲)=𝟎\nabla_{\mathbf{y}}\hat{f}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})=\bm{0}, i.e.,
∇f​(𝐱)+Diag⁡(∇2f​(𝐱))​(𝐲−𝐱)+M2​‖𝐲−𝐱‖2​(𝐲−𝐱)=𝟎.\nabla f(\mathbf{x})+\Diag(\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}))(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{x})+\frac{M}{2}\left\lVert\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{x})=\bm{0}.
(182)
Let rM​(𝐱)=‖𝐱−TM​(𝐱)‖2r_{M}(\mathbf{x})=\left\lVert\mathbf{x}-T_{M}(\mathbf{x})\right\rVert_{2}. For 𝐲=TM​(𝐱)\mathbf{y}=T_{M}(\mathbf{x}) in (182), if we multiply both sides of (182) by TM​(𝐱)−𝐱T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x} we arrive at
∇f(𝐱)T(TM(𝐱)−𝐱)+(TM(𝐱)−𝐱)TDiag(∇2f(𝐱))(TM(𝐱)−𝐱)+M2‖(TM(𝐱)−𝐱)‖23=0.\nabla f(\mathbf{x})^{T}(T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x})+(T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x})^{T}\Diag(\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}))(T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x})+\frac{M}{2}\left\lVert(T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x})\right\rVert_{2}^{3}=0.
(183)
Lemma 14.
For any 𝐱∈F\mathbf{x}\in\pazocal{F} with f⁡(𝐱)≤f⁡(𝐱0)f(\mathbf{x})\leq f(\mathbf{x}_{0}),
we have
∇f(𝐱)T(𝐱−TM(𝐱))≥0.\nabla f(\mathbf{x})^{T}(\mathbf{x}-T_{M}(\mathbf{x}))\geq 0.
(184)
Moreover, if M≥23​LHM\geq\frac{2}{3}L_{H} and 𝐱∈int​F\mathbf{x}\in\mathrm{int}\>\pazocal{F}, then
TM​(𝐱)∈𝔏⁡(f⁡(𝐱)).T_{M}(\mathbf{x})\in\mathfrak{L}(f(\mathbf{x})).
(185)
Proof.
Using Corollary 3, we obtain
Diag⁡(∇2f​(𝐱))+M2​‖𝐱−𝐲‖2​𝐈⪰0,\Diag(\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}))+\frac{M}{2}\left\lVert\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}\right\rVert_{2}~\mathbf{I}\succeq 0,
(186)
which when pre-multiplied by (TM​(𝐱)−𝐱)T(T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x})^{T} and post-multiplied by (TM​(𝐱)−𝐱)(T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x}) yields
(TM​(𝐱)−𝐱)T​Diag⁡(∇2f​(𝐱))​(TM​(𝐱)−𝐱)+M2​‖TM​(𝐱)−𝐱‖23≥0.(T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x})^{T}\Diag(\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}))(T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x})+\frac{M}{2}\left\lVert T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}^{3}\geq 0.
(187)
Then, combining (183) with (187) we arrive at (184).
Assumption and Contradiction. We now show that TM​(𝐱)∈𝔏⁡(f⁡(𝐱))T_{M}(\mathbf{x})\in\mathfrak{L}(f(\mathbf{x})). Following the approach of Nesterov and Polyak (2006, Lemma 2), we proceed by contradiction by assuming TM​(𝐱)∉𝔏⁡(f⁡(𝐱))T_{M}(\mathbf{x})\notin\mathfrak{L}(f(\mathbf{x})) for M≥23​LHM\geq\frac{2}{3}L_{H}. We then show that this assumption cannot hold, leading to a contradiction. Thus, we conclude that TM​(𝐱)∈𝔏⁡(f⁡(𝐱))T_{M}(\mathbf{x})\in\mathfrak{L}(f(\mathbf{x})).
By assuming TM​(𝐱)∉𝔏⁡(f⁡(𝐱))T_{M}(\mathbf{x})\notin\mathfrak{L}(f(\mathbf{x})), there exist
𝐲α=(1−α)​𝐱+α​TM​(𝐱),\mathbf{y}_{\alpha}=(1-\alpha)\>\mathbf{x}+\alpha\>T_{M}(\mathbf{x}),
(188)
with α∈[0,1]\alpha\in[0,1], such that
f⁡(𝐲α)>f⁡(𝐱).f(\mathbf{y}_{\alpha})>f(\mathbf{x}).
(189)
Using the upper bound of (155) with 𝐲=𝐲α\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{y}_{\alpha} we obtain
f(𝐲α)≤f(𝐱)+∇f(𝐱)T(𝐲α−𝐱)+12(𝐲α−𝐱)TDiag(∇2f(𝐱))(𝐲α−𝐱)+LH6‖𝐲α−𝐱‖23=(188)f(𝐱)+α∇f(𝐱)T(TM(𝐱)−𝐱)+α22(TM(𝐱)−𝐱)TDiag(∇2f(𝐱))(TM(𝐱)−𝐱)+α3​LH6‖TM(𝐱)−𝐱‖23,f(\mathbf{y}_{\alpha})\leq f(\mathbf{x})+\nabla f(\mathbf{x})^{T}(\mathbf{y}_{\alpha}-\mathbf{x})+\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{y}_{\alpha}-\mathbf{x})^{T}\Diag(\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}))(\mathbf{y}_{\alpha}-\mathbf{x})+\frac{L_{H}}{6}\left\lVert\mathbf{y}_{\alpha}-\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}^{3}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{eq:ya})}}{{=}}\\
f(\mathbf{x})+\alpha\nabla f(\mathbf{x})^{T}(T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x})+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{2}(T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x})^{T}\Diag(\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}))(T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x})+\frac{\alpha^{3}L_{H}}{6}\left\lVert T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}^{3},
(190)
which using (183) implies
f⁡(𝐲α)−f⁡(𝐱)≤−(α−α22)∇f(𝐱)T(𝐱−TM(𝐱))⏟≥0​ as ​α∈[0,1]​ and​(196)−α22​(M2−α​LH3)​‖TM​(𝐱)−𝐱‖23.f(\mathbf{y}_{\alpha})-f(\mathbf{x})\leq-\underbrace{\left(\alpha-\frac{\alpha^{2}}{2}\right)\nabla f(\mathbf{x})^{T}(\mathbf{x}-T_{M}(\mathbf{x}))}_{\geq 0\text{ as }\alpha\in[0,1]\text{ and}~(\ref{eq:ffhatTMIneq1})}-\frac{\alpha^{2}}{2}\left(\frac{M}{2}-\frac{\alpha L_{H}}{3}\right)\left\lVert T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}^{3}.
(191)
For α≤1\alpha\leq 1, we have
M2−α​LH3≥M2−LH3,\frac{M}{2}-\frac{\alpha L_{H}}{3}\geq\frac{M}{2}-\frac{L_{H}}{3},
(192)
which by using our assumption M≥23​LHM\geq\frac{2}{3}L_{H} it is implied that f⁡(𝐲α)≤f⁡(𝐱)f(\mathbf{y}_{\alpha})\leq f(\mathbf{x}) in (191). However, f⁡(𝐲α)≤f⁡(𝐱)f(\mathbf{y}_{\alpha})\leq f(\mathbf{x}) contradicts (189) for M≥23​LHM\geq\frac{2}{3}L_{H}, which in turn leads to (185), and the proof is complete.
∎
Lemma 15.
If TM​(𝐱)∈FT_{M}(\mathbf{x})\in\pazocal{F} then
‖∇f​(TM​(𝐱))‖2≤LH+M2​rM2​(𝐱).\left\lVert\nabla f(T_{M}(\mathbf{x}))\right\rVert_{2}\leq\frac{L_{H}+M}{2}r_{M}^{2}(\mathbf{x}).
(193)
Proof.
Setting 𝐲=TM​(𝐱)\mathbf{y}=T_{M}(\mathbf{x}) in (154) and (182) we get
‖∇f​(TM​(𝐱))−∇f​(𝐱)−Diag⁡(∇2f​(𝐱))​(TM​(𝐱)−𝐱)‖2≤LH2​‖TM​(𝐱)−𝐱‖2\left\lVert\nabla f(T_{M}(\mathbf{x}))-\nabla f(\mathbf{x})-\Diag(\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}))(T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x})\right\rVert_{2}\leq\frac{L_{H}}{2}\left\lVert T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}
(194)
and
‖∇f​(𝐱)+Diag⁡(∇2f​(𝐱))​(TM​(𝐱)−𝐱)‖2=M2​‖TM​(𝐱)−𝐱‖22=M2​rM2​(𝐱),\left\lVert\nabla f(\mathbf{x})+\Diag(\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}))(T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x})\right\rVert_{2}=\frac{M}{2}\left\lVert T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}=\frac{M}{2}r_{M}^{2}(\mathbf{x}),
(195)
respectively. Then, combining (194) with (195) and the definition of the reverse triangle inequality,
we arrive at (193) and the proof is complete.
∎
Lemma 16.
For any 𝐱∈F\mathbf{x}\in\pazocal{F} we have
f¯M​(𝐱)≤min𝐲⁡(M+LH6​‖𝐲−𝐱‖23+f⁡(𝐲))\bar{f}_{M}(\mathbf{x})\leq\min_{\mathbf{y}}\left(\frac{M+L_{H}}{6}\left\lVert\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}^{3}+f(\mathbf{y})\right)
(196)
and
f⁡(𝐱)−f¯M​(𝐱)≥M12​rM3​(𝐱).f(\mathbf{x})-\bar{f}_{M}(\mathbf{x})\geq\frac{M}{12}r_{M}^{3}(\mathbf{x}).
(197)
Moreover, if M≥LHM\geq L_{H}, then TM​(𝐱)∈FT_{M}(\mathbf{x})\in\pazocal{F} and
f⁡(TM​(𝐱))≤f¯M​(𝐱).f(T_{M}(\mathbf{x}))\leq\bar{f}_{M}(\mathbf{x}).
(198)
Proof.
In the following, we have used the relaxed condition M≥LHM\geq L_{H} as M≥LH>23​LHM\geq L_{H}>\frac{2}{3}L_{H}. Note that the relaxed condition M≥LHM\geq L_{H} also satisfies Lemma 14. From the lower and upper bound of (155) we have
f^​(𝐱,𝐲)≤M+LH6​‖𝐲−𝐱‖23+f⁡(𝐲)\hat{f}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\leq\frac{M+L_{H}}{6}\left\lVert\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}^{3}+f(\mathbf{y})
(199)
and
f​(𝐲)≤f^​(𝐱,𝐲),f(\mathbf{y})\leq\hat{f}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}),
(200)
respectively. Thus, we have
f⁡(𝐲)≤f^​(𝐱,𝐲)≤M+LH6​‖𝐲−𝐱‖23+f⁡(𝐲).f(\mathbf{y})\leq\hat{f}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\leq\frac{M+L_{H}}{6}\left\lVert\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}^{3}+f(\mathbf{y}).
(201)
Minimizing (201) all sides w.r.t. 𝐲\mathbf{y} yields
min𝐲⁡f⁡(𝐲)≤min𝐲⁡f^​(𝐱,𝐲)≤min𝐲⁡(M+LH6​‖𝐲−𝐱‖23+f⁡(𝐲)).\min_{\mathbf{y}}f(\mathbf{y})\leq\min_{\mathbf{y}}\hat{f}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\leq\min_{\mathbf{y}}\left(\frac{M+L_{H}}{6}\left\lVert\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}^{3}+f(\mathbf{y})\right).
(202)
which in turn using 𝐲=TM​(𝐱)\mathbf{y}=T_{M}(\mathbf{x}) yields
f⁡(TM​(𝐱))≤f^​(𝐱,TM​(𝐱))≤min𝐲⁡(M+LH6​‖𝐲−𝐱‖23+f⁡(𝐲)).f(T_{M}(\mathbf{x}))\leq\hat{f}(\mathbf{x},T_{M}(\mathbf{x}))\leq\min_{\mathbf{y}}\left(\frac{M+L_{H}}{6}\left\lVert\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}^{3}+f(\mathbf{y})\right).
(203)
Additionally, using (181) in (203) we obtain (196). Note that (196) aligns with the results presented in Nesterov and Polyak (2006, Lemma 4), with the distinction that Diag⁡(∇2f​(𝐱))\Diag(\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x})) is used in place of ∇2f​(𝐱)\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}). From the LHS of (203) and (178) we obtain
f⁡(𝐱)−f⁡(TM​(𝐱))≥f⁡(𝐱)−f^​(𝐱,TM​(𝐱))=−∇f(𝐱)T(TM(𝐱)−𝐱)−12(TM(𝐱)−𝐱)TDiag(∇2f(𝐱))(TM(𝐱)−𝐱)−M6‖TM(𝐱)−𝐱‖23.f(\mathbf{x})-f(T_{M}(\mathbf{x}))\geq f(\mathbf{x})-\hat{f}(\mathbf{x},T_{M}(\mathbf{x}))\\
=-\nabla f(\mathbf{x})^{T}(T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x})-\frac{1}{2}(T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x})^{T}\Diag(\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}))(T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x})-\frac{M}{6}\left\lVert T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}^{3}.
(204)
In addition, from (183) we have
−12(TM(𝐱)−𝐱)TDiag(∇2f(𝐱))(TM(𝐱)−𝐱)=12∇f(𝐱)T(TM(𝐱)−𝐱)+M4‖TM(𝐱)−𝐱‖23,-\frac{1}{2}(T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x})^{T}\Diag(\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}))(T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x})=\frac{1}{2}\nabla f(\mathbf{x})^{T}(T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x})+\frac{M}{4}\left\lVert T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x}\right\rVert_{2}^{3},
(205)
which combined with (204) and (181) gives
f(𝐱)−f¯M(𝐱)≥−12∇f(𝐱)T(TM(𝐱)−𝐱)+M12rM3(𝐱),f(\mathbf{x})-\bar{f}_{M}(\mathbf{x})\geq-\frac{1}{2}\nabla f(\mathbf{x})^{T}(T_{M}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{x})+\frac{M}{12}r_{M}^{3}(\mathbf{x}),
(206)
which in turn combined with (184) yields (197). To conclude the proof, setting 𝐲=TM​(𝐱)\mathbf{y}=T_{M}(\mathbf{x}) in the LHS of (201) and using (181) we obtain (198).
∎
Lemma 17.
If 𝐱∈F\mathbf{x}\in\pazocal{F} then
μMi​(𝐱i+1)=Δmax⁡{2LH+Mi​‖∇f​(𝐱i+1)‖2,−22​LH+Mi​λmin​(Diag⁡(∇2f​(𝐱i+1)))}.\mu_{M_{i}}(\mathbf{x}_{i+1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\max\Biggl\{\sqrt{\frac{2}{L_{H}+M_{i}}\left\lVert\nabla f(\mathbf{x}_{i+1})\right\rVert_{2}},-\frac{2}{2L_{H}+M_{i}}\lambda_{\text{min}}(\Diag(\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}_{i+1})))\Biggr\}.
(207)
Proof.
From (193) we have
2LH+M​‖∇f​(𝐱i+1)‖2≤‖𝐱i+1−𝐱i‖2.\sqrt{\frac{2}{L_{H}+M}\left\lVert\nabla f(\mathbf{x}_{i+1})\right\rVert_{2}}\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{x}_{i+1}-\mathbf{x}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}.
(208)
(186) implies
Diag⁡(∇2f​(𝐱))−LH​‖𝐱−𝐲‖2​𝐈+M2​‖𝐱−𝐲‖2​𝐈⪰−LH​‖𝐱−𝐲‖2​𝐈⇔λmin​(Diag⁡(∇2f​(𝐱)))−LH​‖𝐱−𝐲‖2≥−(M2+LH)​‖𝐱−𝐲‖2,\Diag(\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}))-L_{H}\left\lVert\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}\right\rVert_{2}\>\mathbf{I}+\frac{M}{2}\left\lVert\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}\right\rVert_{2}\>\mathbf{I}\succeq-L_{H}\left\lVert\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}\right\rVert_{2}\>\mathbf{I}\Leftrightarrow\\
\lambda_{\text{min}}\left(\Diag(\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}))\right)-L_{H}\left\lVert\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}\right\rVert_{2}\geq-\left(\frac{M}{2}+L_{H}\right)\left\lVert\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}\right\rVert_{2},
(209)
which by setting 𝐲=𝐱t+1\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{x}_{t+1} and 𝐱=𝐱t\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{x}_{t} yields
λmin​(Diag⁡(∇2f​(𝐱t)))−LH​‖𝐱t−𝐱t+1‖22≥−(M2+LH)​‖𝐱t−𝐱t+1‖2.\lambda_{\text{min}}\left(\Diag(\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}_{t}))\right)-L_{H}\left\lVert\mathbf{x}_{t}-\mathbf{x}_{t+1}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\geq-\left(\frac{M}{2}+L_{H}\right)\left\lVert\mathbf{x}_{t}-\mathbf{x}_{t+1}\right\rVert_{2}.
(210)
Combining Nesterov (2018, Corrolary 1.2.3) with Lemma 9 for 𝐲=𝐱t+1\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{x}_{t+1} and 𝐱=𝐱t\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{x}_{t} yields
λmin​(Diag⁡(∇2f​(𝐱t+1)))≥λmin​(Diag⁡(∇2f​(𝐱t)))−LH​‖𝐱t−𝐱t+1‖2,\lambda_{\text{min}}\left(\Diag(\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}_{t+1}))\right)\geq\lambda_{\text{min}}\left(\Diag(\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}_{t}))\right)-L_{H}\left\lVert\mathbf{x}_{t}-\mathbf{x}_{t+1}\right\rVert_{2},
(211)
which when combined with (210) gives
λmin​(Diag⁡(∇2f​(𝐱t+1)))≥−(M2+LH)​‖𝐱t−𝐱t+1‖2,\lambda_{\text{min}}\left(\Diag(\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}_{t+1}))\right)\geq-\left(\frac{M}{2}+L_{H}\right)\left\lVert\mathbf{x}_{t}-\mathbf{x}_{t+1}\right\rVert_{2},
(212)
and, in turn, implies
−22​LH+M​λmin​(Diag⁡(∇2f​(𝐱t+1)))≤‖𝐱t−𝐱t+1‖2,-\frac{2}{2L_{H}+M}\lambda_{\text{min}}\left(\Diag(\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}_{t+1}))\right)\leq\left\lVert\mathbf{x}_{t}-\mathbf{x}_{t+1}\right\rVert_{2},
(213)
In order to obtain an (ϵg,ϵH)(\epsilon_{g},\epsilon_{H})-stationary point, we need ‖𝐱t−𝐱t+1‖2≤ϵg\left\lVert\mathbf{x}_{t}-\mathbf{x}_{t+1}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\epsilon_{g} and ‖𝐱t−𝐱t+1‖2≤ϵH\left\lVert\mathbf{x}_{t}-\mathbf{x}_{t+1}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\epsilon_{H} in (208) and (213), respectively, which yields (207), and the proof is complete.
∎
Lemma 18.
For any 𝐱∈F\mathbf{x}\in\pazocal{F} we have
μM​(TM​(𝐱))≤rM​(𝐱).\mu_{M}(T_{M}(\mathbf{x}))\leq r_{M}(\mathbf{x}).
(214)
Proof.
Adapting Nesterov (2018, Corollary 1.2.2) in our context yields
Diag⁡(∇2f​(TM​(𝐱)))⪰Diag⁡(∇2f​(𝐱))−rM​(𝐱)​LH​𝐈.\Diag(\nabla^{2}f(T_{M}(\mathbf{x})))\succeq\Diag(\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}))-r_{M}(\mathbf{x})L_{H}\mathbf{I}.
(215)
Combining (215) and (186) gives
Diag⁡(∇2f​(TM​(𝐱)))⪰−(12​M+LH)​rM​(𝐱)​𝐈,\Diag(\nabla^{2}f(T_{M}(\mathbf{x})))\succeq-\left(\frac{1}{2}M+L_{H}\right)r_{M}(\mathbf{x})\mathbf{I},
(216)
which when combined with Lemma 17 yields (214) and the proof is complete.
∎
B.12 Proof of Theorem 23
Let (𝐬i+1,νi+1)(\mathbf{s}_{i+1},\nu_{i+1}) be the output of Algorithm 2 for 𝐁=Diag⁡(∇2f​(𝐱i))\mathbf{B}=\Diag(\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}_{i})), 𝐠=∇f​(𝐱i)\mathbf{g}=\nabla f(\mathbf{x}_{i}). Recall that (𝐬i+1,νi+1)(\mathbf{s}_{i+1},\nu_{i+1}) is a minimizer of (7) and according to Theorem 2 it is also a minimizer of problem (5) where M=νi+1M=\nu_{i+1}. Recall also that 𝐱i+1=𝐱i+𝐬i+1\mathbf{x}_{i+1}=\mathbf{x}_{i}+\mathbf{s}_{i+1} and let the sequence {𝐱i}i≥1\{\mathbf{x}_{i}\}_{i\geq 1} be generated by Algorithm 1.
Next, suppose that Assumption 1 holds, i.e., the objective function f⁡(𝐱)f(\mathbf{x}) is bounded from bellow, f⁡(𝐱)≥flowf(\mathbf{x})\geq f^{\text{low}} for all 𝐱∈F\mathbf{x}\in\pazocal{F}. Then, we continue with the proof of the main result in Theorem 23. From (197), we have
f⁡(𝐱0)−f¯M0​(𝐱0)≥M012​rM03​(𝐱0)f⁡(𝐱1)−f¯M1​(𝐱1)≥M112​rM13​(𝐱1)f⁡(𝐱k−1)−f¯Mk−1​(𝐱k−1)≥Mk−112​rMk−13​(𝐱k−1),\begin{gathered}f(\mathbf{x}_{0})-\bar{f}_{M_{0}}(\mathbf{x}_{0})\geq\frac{M_{0}}{12}r_{M_{0}}^{3}(\mathbf{x}_{0})\\
f(\mathbf{x}_{1})-\bar{f}_{M_{1}}(\mathbf{x}_{1})\geq\frac{M_{1}}{12}r_{M_{1}}^{3}(\mathbf{x}_{1})\\
\vdots\\
f(\mathbf{x}_{k-1})-\bar{f}_{M_{k-1}}(\mathbf{x}_{k-1})\geq\frac{M_{k-1}}{12}r_{M_{k-1}}^{3}(\mathbf{x}_{k-1}),\end{gathered}
(217)
where rMi​(𝐱i)=‖𝐱i−𝐱i+1‖2r_{M_{i}}(\mathbf{x}_{i})=\left\lVert\mathbf{x}_{i}-\mathbf{x}_{i+1}\right\rVert_{2} and
f¯Mi​(𝐱)=Δmin𝐬∈ℝd​mMi​(𝐬).\bar{f}_{M_{i}}(\mathbf{x})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\underset{\mathbf{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\min}~m_{M_{i}}(\mathbf{s}).
(218)
Summing over (217) we get
∑i=0k−1(f⁡(𝐱i)−f¯Mi​(𝐱i))≥∑i=0k−1Mi12​rMi3​(𝐱i).\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\left(f(\mathbf{x}_{i})-\bar{f}_{M_{i}}(\mathbf{x}_{i})\right)\geq\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\frac{M_{i}}{12}r^{3}_{M_{i}}(\mathbf{x}_{i}).
(219)
Then applying f⁡(𝐱i+1)≤f¯Mi​(𝐱i)f(\mathbf{x}_{i+1})\leq\bar{f}_{M_{i}}(\mathbf{x}_{i}) (Lemma 16), we get
∑i=0k−1(f⁡(𝐱i)−f⁡(𝐱i+1))≥∑i=0k−1Mi12​rMi3​(𝐱i),\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\left(f(\mathbf{x}_{i})-f(\mathbf{x}_{i+1})\right)\geq\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\frac{M_{i}}{12}r^{3}_{M_{i}}(\mathbf{x}_{i}),
(220)
which, by applying the telescoping sum, yields
f⁡(𝐱0)−f⁡(𝐱k)≥∑i=0k−1Mi12​rMi3​(𝐱i).f(\mathbf{x}_{0})-f(\mathbf{x}_{k})\geq\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\frac{M_{i}}{12}r^{3}_{M_{i}}(\mathbf{x}_{i}).
(221)
Next, using the lower bound of f⁡(𝐱k)f(\mathbf{x}_{k}), i.e., flowf^{\text{low}} by Assumption 1 and Remark 1, we obtain
f⁡(𝐱0)−flow≥∑i=0k−1Mi12​rMi3​(𝐱i),f(\mathbf{x}_{0})-f^{\text{low}}\geq\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\frac{M_{i}}{12}r^{3}_{M_{i}}(\mathbf{x}_{i}),
(222)
which implies
f⁡(𝐱0)−flow≥k​L012​rL03​(𝐱i)⇔μMi​(𝐱i+1)≤rMi​(𝐱i)≤121/3​(f⁡(𝐱0)−flowk​Mi)1/3,f(\mathbf{x}_{0})-f^{\text{low}}\geq k\frac{L_{0}}{12}r^{3}_{L_{0}}(\mathbf{x}_{i})\Leftrightarrow\mu_{M_{i}}(\mathbf{x}_{i+1})\leq r_{M_{i}}(\mathbf{x}_{i})\leq 12^{1/3}\left(\frac{f(\mathbf{x}_{0})-f^{\text{low}}}{k\>M_{i}}\right)^{1/3},
(223)
where Lemma 18 (Appendix B.11) is applied to get μMi​(𝐱i+1)≤rMi​(𝐱i)\mu_{M_{i}}(\mathbf{x}_{i+1})\leq r_{M_{i}}(\mathbf{x}_{i}). For LH=MiL_{H}=M_{i} and applying the trick 12=3⋅4⇔121/3=31/3⋅(8/2)1/3⇔121/3=(3/2)1/3⋅81/3⇔121/3=(3/2)1/3⋅2⇔121/3=(3/2)1/3⋅8/4≤(3/2)1/3⋅8/312=3\cdot 4\Leftrightarrow 12^{1/3}=3^{1/3}\cdot(8/2)^{1/3}\Leftrightarrow 12^{1/3}=(3/2)^{1/3}\cdot 8^{1/3}\Leftrightarrow 12^{1/3}=(3/2)^{1/3}\cdot 2\Leftrightarrow 12^{1/3}=(3/2)^{1/3}\cdot 8/4\leq(3/2)^{1/3}\cdot 8/3, we arrive at
μLH​(𝐱i+1)≤83​(32​f⁡(𝐱0)−flowLH​k)1/3.\mu_{L_{H}}(\mathbf{x}_{i+1})\leq\frac{8}{3}\left(\frac{3}{2}\frac{f(\mathbf{x}_{0})-f^{\text{low}}}{L_{H}k\>}\right)^{1/3}.
(224)
As in Nesterov and Polyak (2006, Theorem 3), here it is assumed that ∇2f​(𝐱i)\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}_{i}) is positive definite for some i≥0i\geq 0. The latter assumption implies that Diag⁡(∇2f​(𝐱i))\Diag(\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}_{i})) is also positive definite. Then for some i≥0i\geq 0, from (207), we restrict our study to
μLH​(𝐱i+1)=1LH​‖∇f​(𝐱i+1)‖2,\mu_{L_{H}}(\mathbf{x}_{i+1})=\sqrt{\frac{1}{L_{H}}\left\lVert\nabla f(\mathbf{x}_{i+1})\right\rVert_{2}},
(225)
which combined with (224), yields
min0≤i≤k−1⁡‖∇f​(𝐱i+1)‖2≤LH1/3​(83)2​(32​f⁡(𝐱0)−flowk)2/3,\min_{0\leq i\leq k-1}\left\lVert\nabla f(\mathbf{x}_{i+1})\right\rVert_{2}\leq L_{H}^{1/3}\left(\frac{8}{3}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{3}{2}\frac{f(\mathbf{x}_{0})-f^{\text{low}}}{k\>}\right)^{2/3},
(226)
which implies (23). The convergence rate in (23) is used to establish the local convergence rate when the approximate 𝐠i\mathbf{g}_{i} and 𝐁i\mathbf{B}_{i} are used instead of ∇f​(𝐱i)\nabla f(\mathbf{x}_{i}) and ∇2f​(𝐱i)\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}_{i}), respectively. The latter argument is strengthened by Corollary 2, and the proof is complete.
B.13 Vector and Matrix Bernstein Inequalities
For completeness, we restate Kohler and Lucchi (2017, Lemma 18), incorporating corrections for minor typographical errors. Lemma 19 is utilized by Lemma 5. Next, Lemma 20 is introduced and utilized by Lemma 22, which in turn is utilized by Lemma 6. Lemma 21 is also used by Lemma 22.
Lemma 19 (Vector Bernstein Inequality).
Let 𝐱1,𝐱2,…​𝐱n\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2},\dots\mathbf{x}_{n} be independent random vectors of common dimension dd and assume that each one is centered, uniformly bounded, and also the variance is bounded from above, i.e.,
𝔼⁡[𝐱i]=𝟎​ and ​‖𝐱i‖2≤ϑ​ as well as ​𝔼​[‖𝐱i‖22]≤σ2.\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{x}_{i}]=\mathbf{0}\text{ and }\left\lVert\mathbf{x}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\vartheta\text{ as well as }\mathbb{E}[\left\lVert\mathbf{x}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}]\leq\sigma^{2}.
(227)
Let 𝐳=1n​∑i=1n𝐱i\mathbf{z}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{x}_{i}.
Then we have
Pr⁡(‖𝐳‖2≥ϵ)≤exp⁡(−n​ϵ28​σ2+14),\Pr(\left\lVert\mathbf{z}\right\rVert_{2}\geq\epsilon)\leq\exp\left(-n\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}}+\frac{1}{4}\right),
(228)
with 0<ϵ<σ2/ϑ+σ0<\epsilon<\sigma^{2}/\vartheta+\sigma.
Proof.
A proof can be found in (Kohler and Lucchi, 2017, Lemma 18). However, some typographical errors were identified, leading us to reproduce the proof for clarity.
The Vector Bernstein inequality for independent, zero-mean random vectors Gross (2011, Theorem 12) states
Pr⁡(1n​‖∑i=1n𝐱i‖2≥1n​(t+V))≤exp⁡(−t24​V),\Pr\left(\frac{1}{n}\left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{x}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}\geq\frac{1}{n}(t+\sqrt{V})\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}}{4V}\right),
(229)
where V=∑i=1n𝔼⁡[‖𝐱i‖22]V=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[\left\lVert\mathbf{x}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}] is the sum of the traces of the covariance matrices of the centered vectors 𝐱i\mathbf{x}_{i}. Using 𝔼⁡[‖𝐱i‖22]≤σ2\mathbb{E}[\left\lVert\mathbf{x}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}]\leq\sigma^{2} yields V≤n​σ2V\leq n\sigma^{2}.
Note that in (229), the probability condition is scaled by a factor of 1/n1/n to align with the subsequent analysis involving 𝐳\mathbf{z}. Let ϵ=(t+V)/n⇔t=n​ϵ−V\epsilon=(t+\sqrt{V})/n\Leftrightarrow t=n\epsilon-\sqrt{V}. Using (229) we get
Pr⁡(‖𝐳‖2≥ϵ)≤exp⁡(−14​(n​ϵ−VV)2)=exp⁡(−14​(n​ϵV−1)2).\Pr\left(\left\lVert\mathbf{z}\right\rVert_{2}\geq\epsilon\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{n\epsilon-\sqrt{V}}{\sqrt{V}}\right)^{2}\right)=\exp\left(-\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{n\epsilon}{\sqrt{V}}-1\right)^{2}\right).
(230)
We claim that
−14​(n​ϵV−1)2≤−14​n2​ϵ22​V+14\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{n\epsilon}{\sqrt{V}}-1\right)^{2}\leq-\frac{1}{4}\frac{n^{2}\epsilon^{2}}{2V}+\frac{1}{4}
(231)
Indeed, if (231) holds we arrive at a valid inequality
−n2​ϵ2V+2​n​ϵV−1≤−12​n2​ϵ2V+1\displaystyle-\frac{n^{2}\epsilon^{2}}{V}+2\frac{n\epsilon}{\sqrt{V}}-1\leq-\frac{1}{2}\frac{n^{2}\epsilon^{2}}{V}+1
(232)
⇔\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow
(n​ϵ2​V−2)2≥0.\displaystyle\left(\frac{n\epsilon}{\sqrt{2V}}-\sqrt{2}\right)^{2}\geq 0.
Using (231) in (230) gives
Pr⁡(‖𝐳‖2≥ϵ)≤exp⁡(−n​ϵ28​σ2+14),\Pr\left(\left\lVert\mathbf{z}\right\rVert_{2}\geq\epsilon\right)\leq\exp\left(-n\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}}+\frac{1}{4}\right),
(233)
where V≤n​σ2V\leq n\sigma^{2} is used.
According to Gross (2011, Theorem 12), t<V/maxi⁡‖𝐱i‖2t<V/\max_{i}\left\lVert\mathbf{x}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}. For V≤n​σ2V\leq n\sigma^{2} and ‖𝐱i‖2≤ϑ\left\lVert\mathbf{x}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\vartheta gives t<n​σ2/ϑt<n\sigma^{2}/\vartheta. Given V≤n​σ2V\leq n\sigma^{2} and t<n​σ2/ϑt<n\sigma^{2}/\vartheta, we arrive at
n​ϵ=t+V≤n​σ2ϑ+n​σ⇔ϵ≤σ2ϑ+σ,n\epsilon=t+\sqrt{V}\leq\frac{n\sigma^{2}}{\vartheta}+\sqrt{n}\sigma\Leftrightarrow\epsilon\leq\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\vartheta}+\sigma,
(234)
where x<x\sqrt{x}<x with x>1x>1 is used. In addition, it can be shown that Var⁡(𝐳)≤σ2/n\mathrm{Var}(\mathbf{z})\leq\sigma^{2}/n Gross (2011, Theorem 12) establishing (228), which concluded the proof.
∎
Lemma 20.
Let 𝐮i:Ω𝐮i→ℝn\mathbf{u}_{i}:\Omega_{\mathbf{u}_{i}}\to\mathbb{R}^{n} and 𝐯j:Ω𝐯j→ℝm\mathbf{v}_{j}:\Omega_{\mathbf{v}_{j}}\to\mathbb{R}^{m} be independent random vectors for each i,ji,j. Let g:ℝn×ℝm→ℝd×dg:\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}\to\mathbb{R}^{d\times d} be a function that produces random matrices. Then, for any indices (i,j)≠(k,l)(i,j)\neq(k,l), the matrices g⁡(𝐮i,𝐯j)g(\mathbf{u}_{i},\mathbf{v}_{j}) and g⁡(𝐮k,𝐯l)g(\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{v}_{l}) are independent, regardless of whether 𝐮i\mathbf{u}_{i} and 𝐯j\mathbf{v}_{j} come from the same or different distributions.
Proof.
Let two threshold matrices 𝐌\mathbf{M} and 𝐌′\mathbf{M}^{\prime} (which are symmetric d×dd\times d matrices), and consider the probability
P⁡(g⁡(𝐮i,𝐯j) ⋖− 𝐌∩g⁡(𝐮k,𝐯l) ⋖− 𝐌′),P(g(\mathbf{u}_{i},\mathbf{v}_{j})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$\lessdot$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-1.66666pt\raisebox{-2.54375pt}{\hbox{$-$}}\hskip-1.66666pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathbf{M}\cap g(\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{v}_{l})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$\lessdot$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-1.66666pt\raisebox{-2.54375pt}{\hbox{$-$}}\hskip-1.66666pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}),
(235)
using the element-wise comparison operator ⋖− \mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$\lessdot$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-1.66666pt\raisebox{-2.54375pt}{\hbox{$-$}}\hskip-1.66666pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}. Given that 𝐮i:Ω𝐮i→ℝn\mathbf{u}_{i}:\Omega_{\mathbf{u}_{i}}\to\mathbb{R}^{n} and 𝐯j:Ω𝐯j→ℝm\mathbf{v}_{j}:\Omega_{\mathbf{v}_{j}}\to\mathbb{R}^{m} are independent random vectors, and g:ℝn×ℝm→ℝd×dg:\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}\to\mathbb{R}^{d\times d} is a function generating random matrices, we rewrite the event as
{g(𝐮i,𝐯j) ⋖− 𝐌}≡{(ω𝐮i,ω𝐯j)∈Ω𝐮i×Ω𝐯j:g(𝐮i(ω𝐮i),𝐯j(ω𝐯j)) ⋖− 𝐌}≡{(𝐮i,𝐯j)∈ℝn×ℝm:g⁡(𝐮i,𝐯j) ⋖− 𝐌}.\{g(\mathbf{u}_{i},\mathbf{v}_{j})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$\lessdot$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-1.66666pt\raisebox{-2.54375pt}{\hbox{$-$}}\hskip-1.66666pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathbf{M}\}\equiv\{(\omega_{\mathbf{u}_{i}},\omega_{\mathbf{v}_{j}})\in\Omega_{\mathbf{u}_{i}}\times\Omega_{\mathbf{v}_{j}}:g(\mathbf{u}_{i}(\omega_{\mathbf{u}_{i}}),\mathbf{v}_{j}(\omega_{\mathbf{v}_{j}}))\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$\lessdot$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-1.66666pt\raisebox{-2.54375pt}{\hbox{$-$}}\hskip-1.66666pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathbf{M}\}\\
\equiv\{(\mathbf{u}_{i},\mathbf{v}_{j})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}:g(\mathbf{u}_{i},\mathbf{v}_{j})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$\lessdot$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-1.66666pt\raisebox{-2.54375pt}{\hbox{$-$}}\hskip-1.66666pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathbf{M}\}.
(236)
Similarly, we have
{g(𝐮k,𝐯l) ⋖− 𝐌′}≡{(ω𝐮k,ω𝐯l)∈Ω𝐮k×Ω𝐯l:g(𝐮k(ω𝐮k),𝐯l(ω𝐯l)) ⋖− 𝐌′}≡{(𝐮k,𝐯l)∈ℝn×ℝm:g⁡(𝐮k,𝐯l) ⋖− 𝐌′}.\{g(\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{v}_{l})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$\lessdot$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-1.66666pt\raisebox{-2.54375pt}{\hbox{$-$}}\hskip-1.66666pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\}\equiv\{(\omega_{\mathbf{u}_{k}},\omega_{\mathbf{v}_{l}})\in\Omega_{\mathbf{u}_{k}}\times\Omega_{\mathbf{v}_{l}}:g(\mathbf{u}_{k}(\omega_{\mathbf{u}_{k}}),\mathbf{v}_{l}(\omega_{\mathbf{v}_{l}}))\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$\lessdot$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-1.66666pt\raisebox{-2.54375pt}{\hbox{$-$}}\hskip-1.66666pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\}\\
\equiv\{(\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{v}_{l})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}:g(\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{v}_{l})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$\lessdot$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-1.66666pt\raisebox{-2.54375pt}{\hbox{$-$}}\hskip-1.66666pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\}.
(237)
Let
A={(𝐮i,𝐯j)∈ℝn×ℝm:g⁡(𝐮i,𝐯j) ⋖− 𝐌}A=\{(\mathbf{u}_{i},\mathbf{v}_{j})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}:g(\mathbf{u}_{i},\mathbf{v}_{j})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$\lessdot$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-1.66666pt\raisebox{-2.54375pt}{\hbox{$-$}}\hskip-1.66666pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathbf{M}\}
(238)
and
B={(𝐮k,𝐯l)∈ℝn×ℝm:g⁡(𝐮k,𝐯l) ⋖− 𝐌′}.B=\{(\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{v}_{l})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}:g(\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{v}_{l})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$\lessdot$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-1.66666pt\raisebox{-2.54375pt}{\hbox{$-$}}\hskip-1.66666pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\}.
(239)
Then using (236) and (237) in (235), we write
P({g(𝐮i,𝐯j) ⋖− 𝐌}∩{g(𝐮k,𝐯l) ⋖− 𝐌′})=P((𝐮i,𝐯j)∈A∩(𝐮k,𝐯l)∈B).P(\{g(\mathbf{u}_{i},\mathbf{v}_{j})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$\lessdot$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-1.66666pt\raisebox{-2.54375pt}{\hbox{$-$}}\hskip-1.66666pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathbf{M}\}\cap\{g(\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{v}_{l})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$\lessdot$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-1.66666pt\raisebox{-2.54375pt}{\hbox{$-$}}\hskip-1.66666pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\})=P((\mathbf{u}_{i},\mathbf{v}_{j})\in A\cap(\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{v}_{l})\in B).
(240)
Recall that the sequences {𝐮i}\{\mathbf{u}_{i}\} and {𝐯j}\{\mathbf{v}_{j}\} are independent families of random variables which implies that the pairs (𝐮i,𝐯j)(\mathbf{u}_{i},\mathbf{v}_{j}) are formed by drawing independently from these families. Thus, since 𝐮i\mathbf{u}_{i} and 𝐯j\mathbf{v}_{j} are independent for each (i,j)(i,j), and (𝐮k,𝐯l)(\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{v}_{l}) are also independent, we have
P({g(𝐮i,𝐯j) ⋖− 𝐌}∩{g(𝐮k,𝐯l) ⋖− 𝐌′})=P((𝐮i,𝐯j)∈A)P((𝐮k,𝐯l)∈B),P(\{g(\mathbf{u}_{i},\mathbf{v}_{j})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$\lessdot$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-1.66666pt\raisebox{-2.54375pt}{\hbox{$-$}}\hskip-1.66666pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathbf{M}\}\cap\{g(\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{v}_{l})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$\lessdot$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-1.66666pt\raisebox{-2.54375pt}{\hbox{$-$}}\hskip-1.66666pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\})=P((\mathbf{u}_{i},\mathbf{v}_{j})\in A)P((\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{v}_{l})\in B),
(241)
which implies
P({g(𝐮i,𝐯j) ⋖− 𝐌}∩{g(𝐮k,𝐯l) ⋖− 𝐌′})=P(g(𝐮i,𝐯j) ⋖− 𝐌)P(g(𝐮k,𝐯l) ⋖− 𝐌′),P(\{g(\mathbf{u}_{i},\mathbf{v}_{j})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$\lessdot$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-1.66666pt\raisebox{-2.54375pt}{\hbox{$-$}}\hskip-1.66666pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathbf{M}\}\cap\{g(\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{v}_{l})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$\lessdot$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-1.66666pt\raisebox{-2.54375pt}{\hbox{$-$}}\hskip-1.66666pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}\})=P(g(\mathbf{u}_{i},\mathbf{v}_{j})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$\lessdot$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-1.66666pt\raisebox{-2.54375pt}{\hbox{$-$}}\hskip-1.66666pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathbf{M})P(g(\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{v}_{l})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$\lessdot$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-1.66666pt\raisebox{-2.54375pt}{\hbox{$-$}}\hskip-1.66666pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathbf{M}^{\prime}),
(242)
where (236) and (237) are used. Since the joint probability factorizes, this proves that g⁡(𝐮i,𝐯j)g(\mathbf{u}_{i},\mathbf{v}_{j}) and g⁡(𝐮k,𝐯l)g(\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{v}_{l}) are independent whenever (i,j)≠(k,l)(i,j)\neq(k,l), regardless of whether 𝐮i\mathbf{u}_{i} and 𝐯j\mathbf{v}_{j} come from the same or different distributions.
∎
Lemma 21.
Let 𝐗∈ℝd×d\mathbf{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d} be a symmetric mean-zero matrix with ‖𝐗‖≤1\left\lVert\mathbf{X}\right\rVert\leq 1 almost surely. Then,
𝔼⁡[exp⁡(λ​𝐗)]⪯exp⁡(g⁡(λ)​𝔼​[𝐗2]),\mathbb{E}\left[\exp(\lambda\mathbf{X})\right]\preceq\exp\left(g(\lambda)\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}^{2}]\right),
(243)
where g⁡(λ)=eλ−λ−1g(\lambda)=e^{\lambda}-\lambda-1.
Proof.
We refer the reader to (Vershynin, 2018).
∎
Lemma 22 (Matrix Bernstein Inequality).
Let 𝐗i​j=Δg⁡(𝐮i,𝐯j)\mathbf{X}_{ij}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}g(\mathbf{u}_{i},\mathbf{v}_{j}) be d×dd\times d zero-mean random matrices with two independent sources of randomness, 𝐮i\mathbf{u}_{i} and 𝐯j\mathbf{v}_{j}. Also, let {𝐗i​j}i,j=1N,M\{\mathbf{X}_{ij}\}_{i,j=1}^{N,M} be a set of independent random matrices of common dimension d×dd\times d, such that ‖𝐗i​j‖2≤K\left\lVert\mathbf{X}_{ij}\right\rVert_{2}\leq K almost surely for all i,ji,j. Then, for every t≥0t\geq 0, we have
Pr⁡(‖∑i=1N∑j=1M𝐗i​j‖2≥t)≤2​d​exp⁡(−t2/2σ2+K​t/3).\Pr\left(\left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\mathbf{X}_{ij}\right\rVert_{2}\geq t\right)\leq 2d\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}/2}{\sigma^{2}+Kt/3}\right).
(244)
Here, the matrix variance is given by
σ2=‖∑i=1N∑j=1M𝔼⁡[𝐗i​j2]‖2.\sigma^{2}=\left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{X}_{ij}^{2}\right]\right\rVert_{2}.
(245)
In particular, we can express this bound as a mixture of sub-Gaussian and sub-exponential tails, just like in the scalar Bernstein’s inequality:
Pr⁡(‖∑i=1N∑j=1M𝐗i​j‖2≥t)≤2​d​exp⁡(−38​min⁡{t2σ2,tK}).\Pr\left(\left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\mathbf{X}_{ij}\right\rVert_{2}\geq t\right)\leq 2d\exp\left(-\frac{3}{8}\min\left\{\frac{t^{2}}{\sigma^{2}},\frac{t}{K}\right\}\right).
(246)
Proof.
The following analysis is based on (Vershynin, 2018, Theorem 5.4.1).
Reduction of MGF. To bound the norm of the sum
𝐒=Δ∑i=1N∑j=1M𝐗i​j,\mathbf{S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\mathbf{X}_{ij},
(247)
we need to control the largest and smallest eigenvalues of 𝐒\mathbf{S}. We can do this separately. To put this formally, consider the largest eigenvalue
λmax​(𝐒)=Δmaxi⁡λi​(𝐒)\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\max_{i}\lambda_{i}(\mathbf{S})
(248)
and note that
‖𝐒‖2=max⁡|λi​(𝐒)|=max⁡{λmax​(𝐒),λmax​(−𝐒)}\left\lVert\mathbf{S}\right\rVert_{2}=\max|\lambda_{i}(\mathbf{S})|=\max\{\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{S}),\lambda_{\max}(-\mathbf{S})\}
(249)
and
Pr⁡(|λmax​(𝐒)|≥t)=Pr⁡(λmax​(𝐒)≥t)+Pr⁡(λmax​(−𝐒)≥t)−Pr⁡(λmax​(𝐒)≥t​ and ​λmax​(−𝐒)≥t),\Pr(|\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{S})|\geq t)=\Pr(\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{S})\geq t)+\Pr(\lambda_{\max}(-\mathbf{S})\geq t)-\Pr(\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{S})\geq t\text{ and }\lambda_{\max}(-\mathbf{S})\geq t),
(250)
which implies
Pr⁡(|λmax​(𝐒)|≥t)≤Pr⁡(λmax​(𝐒)≥t)+Pr⁡(λmax​(−𝐒)≥t).\Pr(|\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{S})|\geq t)\leq\Pr(\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{S})\geq t)+\Pr(\lambda_{\max}(-\mathbf{S})\geq t).
(251)
To bound λmax​(𝐒)\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{S}), we proceed with computing the moment generating function. We fix λ≥0\lambda\geq 0 and use Markov’s inequality to obtain
Pr⁡(λmax​(𝐒)≥t)=Pr⁡(eλ​λmax​(𝐒)≥eλ​t)≤e−λ​t​𝔼​[eλ​λmax​(𝐒)].\Pr(\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{S})\geq t)=\Pr(e^{\lambda\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{S})}\geq e^{\lambda t})\leq e^{-\lambda t}\>\mathbb{E}[e^{\lambda\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{S})}].
(252)
Since by Vershynin (2018, Definition 5.4.2) the eigenvalues of eλ​𝐒e^{\lambda\mathbf{S}} are eλ​λi​(𝐒)e^{\lambda\lambda_{i}(\mathbf{S})}, we have
E=Δ𝔼⁡[eλ​λmax​(𝐒)]=𝔼⁡[λmax​(eλ​𝐒)].{E}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}[e^{\lambda\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{S})}]=\mathbb{E}[\lambda_{\max}(e^{\lambda\mathbf{S}})].
(253)
Since the eigenvalues of eλ​𝐒e^{\lambda\mathbf{S}} are all positive, the maximum eigenvalue of eλ​𝐒e^{\lambda\mathbf{S}} is bounded by the sum of all eigenvalues, the trace of eλ​𝐒e^{\lambda\mathbf{S}}, which leads to
E≤𝔼⁡[Tr⁡(eλ​𝐒)].{E}\leq\mathbb{E}[\Tr(e^{\lambda\mathbf{S}})].
(254)
Application of Lieb’s inequality. First note that
𝐒=∑i=1N−1∑j=1M−1𝐗i​j+∑i=1N−1𝐗i​M+∑j=1M−1𝐗N​j+𝐗N​M.\mathbf{S}=\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M-1}\mathbf{X}_{ij}+\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\mathbf{X}_{iM}+\sum_{j=1}^{M-1}\mathbf{X}_{Nj}+\mathbf{X}_{NM}.
(255)
To prepare the application of Lieb’s inequality in Vershynin (2018, Lemma 5.4.9), let us separate the last term from the sum 𝐒\mathbf{S}
E[≤𝔼Tr(exp(∑i=1N−1∑j=1M−1λ𝐗i​j+∑i=1N−1λ𝐗i​M+∑j=1M−1λ𝐗N​j+λ𝐗N​M))].{E}\Biggl[\leq\mathbb{E}\,\Tr\Biggl(\exp\Biggl(\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M-1}\lambda\mathbf{X}_{ij}+\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\lambda\mathbf{X}_{iM}+\sum_{j=1}^{M-1}\lambda\mathbf{X}_{Nj}+\lambda\mathbf{X}_{NM}\Biggr)\Biggr)\Biggr].
(256)
Conditioning on {𝐗i​j}i,j=1N−1,M−1\{\mathbf{X}_{ij}\}_{i,j=1}^{N-1,M-1} and applying Vershynin (2018, Lemma 5.4.9) for the fixed matrix
𝐇=Δ∑i=1N−1∑j=1M−1λ​𝐗i​j+∑i=1N−1λ​𝐗i​M+∑j=1M−1λ​𝐗N​j\mathbf{H}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M-1}\lambda\mathbf{X}_{ij}+\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\lambda\mathbf{X}_{iM}+\sum_{j=1}^{M-1}\lambda\mathbf{X}_{Nj}
(257)
and the random matrix 𝐙=Δλ​𝐗N​M\mathbf{Z}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\lambda\mathbf{X}_{NM}, we obtain
E≤𝔼{𝐗i​j}i,j=1N,M​[Tr⁡(exp⁡(∑i=1N−1∑j=1M−1λ​𝐗i​j+∑i=1N−1λ​𝐗i​M+∑j=1M−1λ​𝐗N​j+λ​𝐗N​M))]≤𝔼{𝐗i​j}i,j=1N−1,M−1​[𝔼𝐗N​M​[Tr⁡(exp⁡(∑i=1N−1∑j=1M−1λ​𝐗i​j+∑i=1N−1λ​𝐗i​M+∑j=1M−1λ​𝐗N​j+λ​𝐗N​M))]]≤𝔼{𝐗i​j}i,j=1N−1,M−1​[Tr⁡(exp⁡(∑i=1N−1∑j=1M−1λ​𝐗i​j+∑i=1N−1λ​𝐗i​M+∑j=1M−1λ​𝐗N​j+log⁡𝔼𝐗N​M​eλ​𝐗N​M))].{E}\leq\mathbb{E}_{\{\mathbf{X}_{ij}\}_{i,j=1}^{N,M}}\Biggl[\Tr\Biggl(\exp\Biggl(\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M-1}\lambda\mathbf{X}_{ij}+\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\lambda\mathbf{X}_{iM}+\sum_{j=1}^{M-1}\lambda\mathbf{X}_{Nj}+\lambda\mathbf{X}_{NM}\Biggr)\Biggr)\Biggr]\\
\leq\mathbb{E}_{\{\mathbf{X}_{ij}\}_{i,j=1}^{N-1,M-1}}\Biggl[\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{X}_{NM}}\Biggl[\Tr\Biggl(\exp\Biggl(\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M-1}\lambda\mathbf{X}_{ij}+\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\lambda\mathbf{X}_{iM}+\sum_{j=1}^{M-1}\lambda\mathbf{X}_{Nj}+\lambda\mathbf{X}_{NM}\Biggr)\Biggr)\Biggr]\Biggr]\\
\leq\mathbb{E}_{\{\mathbf{X}_{ij}\}_{i,j=1}^{N-1,M-1}}\Biggl[\Tr\Biggl(\exp\Biggl(\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M-1}\lambda\mathbf{X}_{ij}+\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\lambda\mathbf{X}_{iM}+\sum_{j=1}^{M-1}\lambda\mathbf{X}_{Nj}+\log\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{X}_{NM}}e^{\lambda\mathbf{X}_{NM}}\Biggr)\Biggr)\Biggr].
(258)
We continue similarly: separate the next term λ​𝐗N−1,M−1\lambda\mathbf{X}_{N-1,M-1} from the remaining sum and apply Vershynin (2018, Lemma 5.4.9) again for 𝐙=λ​𝐗N−1,M−1\mathbf{Z}=\lambda\mathbf{X}_{N-1,M-1}. Repeating this process N​MNM times, we obtain
Pr⁡(λmax​(𝐒)≥t)≤Tr⁡(e−λ​t​exp⁡(∑i=1N∑j=1Mlog⁡𝔼​exp⁡λ​𝐗i​j)).\Pr(\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{S})\geq t)\leq\Tr\left(e^{-\lambda t}\exp\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\log\mathbb{E}\,\exp{\lambda\mathbf{X}_{ij}}\right)\right).
(259)
MGF of the individual terms.
It remains to bound the matrix-valued moment generating function 𝔼​eλ​𝐗i​j\mathbb{E}\,e^{\lambda\mathbf{X}_{ij}} for each term 𝐗i​j\mathbf{X}_{ij}. We now use Lemma 21.
Completion of the proof. Using Lemma 21, we obtain
𝔼⁡[exp⁡(λ​𝐗i​j/K)]⪯exp⁡(g⁡(λ)​𝔼​[𝐗i​j2]/K2)⇔∏i,j=1N,M𝔼⁡[exp⁡(λ​𝐗i​j/K)]⪯∏i,j=1N,Mexp⁡(g⁡(λ)​𝔼​[𝐗i​j2]/K2)⇔∏i,j=1N,M𝔼⁡[exp⁡(λ​𝐗i​j/K)]⪯exp⁡(g⁡(λ)​∑i,j=1N,M𝔼⁡[𝐗i​j2]/K2),\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\mathbf{X}_{ij}/K\right)\right]\preceq\exp\left(g(\lambda)\>\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{ij}^{2}]/K^{2}\right)\Leftrightarrow\prod_{i,j=1}^{N,M}\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\mathbf{X}_{ij}/K\right)\right]\preceq\prod_{i,j=1}^{N,M}\exp\left(g(\lambda)\>\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{ij}^{2}]/K^{2}\right)\\
\Leftrightarrow\prod_{i,j=1}^{N,M}\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\mathbf{X}_{ij}/K\right)\right]\preceq\exp\left(g(\lambda)\>\sum_{i,j=1}^{N,M}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{ij}^{2}]/K^{2}\right),
(260)
which implies
∏i,j=1N,M𝔼⁡[exp⁡(λ​𝐗i​j/K)]⪯exp⁡(g⁡(λ)​∑i,j=1N,M𝔼⁡[𝐗i​j2]/K2).\prod_{i,j=1}^{N,M}\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\mathbf{X}_{ij}/K\right)\right]\preceq\exp\left(g(\lambda)\>\sum_{i,j=1}^{N,M}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{ij}^{2}]/K^{2}\right).
(261)
Also, given that 𝐗i​j\mathbf{X}_{ij} are independent, we have
∏i,j=1N,M𝔼⁡[exp⁡(λ​𝐗i​j/K)]=exp⁡(log⁡(∏i,j=1N,M𝔼​exp⁡(λ​𝐗i​j/K)))=exp⁡(∑i,j=1N,Mlog⁡𝔼​exp⁡(λ​𝐗i​j/K)),\prod_{i,j=1}^{N,M}\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\lambda\mathbf{X}_{ij}/K\right)\right]=\exp\left(\log\left(\prod_{i,j=1}^{N,M}\mathbb{E}\exp\left(\lambda\mathbf{X}_{ij}/K\right)\right)\right)=\\
\exp\left(\sum_{i,j=1}^{N,M}\log\mathbb{E}\exp\left(\lambda\mathbf{X}_{ij}/K\right)\right),
(262)
which combined with (261)
exp⁡(∑i,j=1N,Mlog⁡𝔼​exp⁡(λ​𝐗i​j/K))⪯exp⁡(g⁡(λ)​∑i,j=1N,M𝔼⁡[𝐗i​j2]/K2),\exp\left(\sum_{i,j=1}^{N,M}\log\mathbb{E}\exp\left(\lambda\mathbf{X}_{ij}/K\right)\right)\preceq\exp\left(g(\lambda)\>\sum_{i,j=1}^{N,M}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}_{ij}^{2}]/K^{2}\right),
(263)
and applying the trace to both sides yields
Tr⁡(exp⁡(∑i,j=1N,Mlog⁡𝔼​exp⁡(λ​𝐗i​j/K)))≤Tr⁡(exp⁡(g⁡(λ)​𝐙~/K2)),\Tr\left(\exp\left(\sum_{i,j=1}^{N,M}\log\mathbb{E}\exp\left(\lambda\mathbf{X}_{ij}/K\right)\right)\right)\leq\Tr\left(\exp\left(g(\lambda)\>\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}/K^{2}\right)\right),
(264)
where 𝐙~=Δ𝔼⁡[∑i=1N∑j=1M𝐗i​j2]\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\mathbf{X}_{ij}^{2}\right]. Since the trace of exp⁡(g⁡(λ)​𝐙~/K2)\exp(g(\lambda)\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}/K^{2}) is a sum of dd positive eigenvalues, it is bounded by dd times the maximum eigenvalue and using Vershynin (2018, Definition 5.4.2), we obtain
Tr⁡(exp⁡(g⁡(λ)​𝐙~/K2))≤d​λmax​(exp⁡(g⁡(λ)​𝐙~/K2))=d​exp⁡(g⁡(λ)​λmax​(𝐙~/K2))=d​exp⁡(g⁡(λ)​‖𝐙~‖/K2)=d​exp⁡(g⁡(λ)​σ2/K2).\Tr\left(\exp\left(g(\lambda)\>\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}/K^{2}\right)\right)\leq d\>\lambda_{\max}\left(\exp\left(g(\lambda)\>\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}/K^{2}\right)\right)=d\>\exp\left(g(\lambda)\>\lambda_{\max}(\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}/K^{2})\right)\\
=d\>\exp\left(g(\lambda)\>\|\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}\|/K^{2}\right)=d\>\exp\left(g(\lambda)\>\sigma^{2}/K^{2}\right).
(265)
Combining (259) and (265) we get
Pr⁡(λmax​(𝐒)≥K​t)≤e−λ​t​Tr⁡(exp⁡(∑i=1N∑j=1Mlog⁡𝔼​exp​λ​𝐗i​j/K))≤d​exp⁡(−λ​t+g⁡(λ)​σ2/K2),\Pr(\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{S})\geq Kt)\leq e^{-\lambda t}\Tr\left(\exp\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\log\mathbb{E}\,\exp{\lambda\mathbf{X}_{ij}/K}\right)\right)\leq d\>\exp\left(-\lambda t+g(\lambda)\>\sigma^{2}/K^{2}\right),
(266)
which implies
Pr⁡(λmax​(𝐒)≥t)≤d​exp⁡(−λK​t+g⁡(λ)K2​σ2).\Pr(\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{S})\geq t)\leq d\>\exp\left(-\frac{\lambda}{K}t+\frac{g(\lambda)}{K^{2}}\sigma^{2}\right).
(267)
Minimizing over λ>0\lambda>0, the minimum occurs at
λ=log⁡(1+K​tσ2),t≥0.\lambda=\log\left(1+\frac{Kt}{\sigma^{2}}\right),\quad t\geq 0.
(268)
Plugging this into the bound, we get
Pr⁡(λmax​(𝐒)≥t)≤d​exp⁡(−σ2K2​h​(K​tσ2)),\Pr\left(\lambda_{\max}\left(\mathbf{S}\right)\geq t\right)\leq d\>\exp\left(-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{K^{2}}\>h\left(\frac{Kt}{\sigma^{2}}\right)\right),
(269)
where
h⁡(u)=(1+u)​log⁡(1+u)−u,for ​u>0.h(u)=(1+u)\log(1+u)-u,\quad\text{for }u>0.
(270)
We know that Boucheron et al. (2013, Exercise 2.8)
h⁡(u)≥u22​(1+u/3),h(u)\geq\frac{u^{2}}{2(1+u/3)},
(271)
with u>0u>0 and thus
Pr⁡(λmax​(𝐒)≥t)≤d​exp⁡(−σ2K2​u22​(1+u/3)),\Pr\left(\lambda_{\max}\left(\mathbf{S}\right)\geq t\right)\leq d\>\exp\left(-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{K^{2}}\>\frac{u^{2}}{2(1+u/3)}\right),
(272)
where u=K​tσ2u=\frac{Kt}{\sigma^{2}}. Substituting uu in (272), we obtain
Pr⁡(λmax​(𝐒)≥t)≤d​exp⁡(−t2/2σ2+K​t/3).\Pr\left(\lambda_{\max}\left(\mathbf{S}\right)\geq t\right)\leq d\>\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}/2}{\sigma^{2}+Kt/3}\right).
(273)
Following similar steps with −𝐒-\mathbf{S} instead of 𝐒\mathbf{S} and using (251), yields
P⁡(|λmax​(𝐒)|≥t)≤{2​d​exp⁡(−3​t28​σ2),t≤σ2/K2​d​exp⁡(−3​t8​K),t>σ2/K.P(|\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{S})|\geq t)\leq\begin{cases}2d\exp\left(\frac{-3t^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}}\right),\quad t\leq\sigma^{2}/K\\
2d\exp\left(\frac{-3t}{8K}\right),\quad t>\sigma^{2}/K.\end{cases}
(274)
Intuitively, for small tt, i.e., t≤σ2/Kt\leq\sigma^{2}/K, we have a sub-Gaussian bound, while for large tt, i.e., t>σ2/Kt>\sigma^{2}/K, we have a sub-exponential bound. Looking for the tightest bound, we may write
P⁡(|λmax​(𝐒)|≥t)≤2​d​exp⁡(−38​min⁡{t2σ2,tK}).P(|\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{S})|\geq t)\leq 2d\exp\left(-\frac{3}{8}\min\left\{\frac{t^{2}}{\sigma^{2}},\frac{t}{K}\right\}\right).
(275)
∎

Instructions for reporting errors
We are continuing to improve HTML versions of papers, and your feedback helps enhance accessibility and mobile
support. To report errors in the HTML that will help us improve conversion and rendering, choose any of the
methods listed below:
Click the "Report Issue" (
) button, located in the page header.
Tip: You can select the relevant text first, to include it in your report.
Our team has already identified the following issues. We appreciate your time reviewing and reporting rendering errors we
may not have found yet. Your efforts will help us improve the HTML versions for all readers, because disability
should not be a barrier to accessing research. Thank you for your continued support in championing open access for
all.
Have a free development cycle? Help support accessibility at arXiv! Our collaborators at LaTeXML maintain a list of packages that need conversion, and welcome developer contributions.
